2026-09-09⭐ UBER(/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED, BUT THE AV THREAT ON THIS PAGE WENT FROM QUALITATIVE TO QUANTIFIED AND DATED.** 🔴 **NET-NEW (Barclays · Ross Sandler, "'27 Setting Up As 'The Year Of The Robotaxi'", 09-08): **Waymo is set up to gain ~4% of the US ridehail industry in '27**; **AV supply points to Waymo maintaining 3x-5x Uber's fleet size through '29**; **Waymo's fleet could expand 4x to 26K+ vehicles across close to 30 US cities by end-2027**; and the conversion mechanic that makes it trackable — **"every 10K Waymo AVs, completing 25x trips/day, could take 3% market share from Uber and Lyft in the US in 2027"**. Barclays: "if UBER and LYFT see gross bookings DECELERATE IN 2027 as AV ramps, we think shares could face additional pressure, but some of this is already expected by the investment community."** **THE OFFSETS, BOTH RECORDED: the TAM argument is CONCEDED but DEFERRED — "AV is expansionary to ridehail TAM… and we agree, BUT NOT UNTIL THE COST CURVE IMPROVES MEANINGFULLY, WHICH WE DON'T SEE UNTIL 2030 ONWARD"; and five frictions are named — Uber lobbying to slow AV deployments, AV "not often fully unsupervised L4", Cybercab as the first and currently only low-cost AV in the US, the remote-operator ratio, and first/last mile being hard in AV. 2026-09-09⭐ SPOT(/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED (Barclays holds Overweight, PT $565).** 🔴 **NET-NEW AND MATERIAL: Barclays published the first quantitative framework for audio-streaming inference costs on this page, and the structure of the argument is what makes it usable. **Remix and Personal Podcasts are monetised through paid add-ons or usage-based credits (a direct offset); AI DJ and Talk to Spotify are bundled within Premium (the cost is absorbed).** The conclusion, verbatim: **"the SHAPE of the cost curve matters more than its LEVEL. The two cost buckets WITH revenue attached are SELF-LIMITING BY CONSTRUCTION as costs cannot outrun revenue in either. The only bucket WITHOUT a revenue offset is also the SMALLEST, and the one MOST EXPOSED TO UNIT-COST DEFLATION"** — hence inference cost is "unlikely to derail the margin trajectory" and management's **"neutral to accretive" guidance is judged CREDIBLE**. Barclays expects the debate to move **"from cost to adoption, as rest of the major labels sign AI music deals with Spotify."**** 🔴 **AND THE ADOPTION CATALYST ARRIVED IN THE SAME WINDOW: Suno inked an AI-licensing deal with WMG (and BMG), rolling out v6 models trained in partnership with them and PAYING LABELS AND PUBLISHERS ROYALTIES when the models are used, with artist-name prompting blocked. Bernstein reads a SPOT deal as "likely imminent", positioning Spotify as "the preferred platform to engage with AI music", and sizes the WMG training-revenue stream alone at MID-$100MNs next year.** **PRICING-POWER CORROBORATION FROM AN UNRELATED DATASET: MS finds WMG US subscriber growth of +9.2% versus a +6.4% industry rate and reads it as "constructive for SPOT pricing power with paid user share gains DURING A PERIOD OF TAKING PRICE in Feb", with upside to a ~7% FY26 US streaming growth forecast as major-platform pricing rolls through 2H.** ⚠️ **STANDING CAVEAT FOR THIS PAGE: SPOT is one of the five names on this wiki with a TRADING-vs-FUNDAMENTALS CURRENCY MISMATCH — never divide `px` or `pt` by `eps` here.** Cross-filed [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).

SemiAnalysis: "TPUv7 Ironwood, running the new native TorchTPU stack, is already showing up to ~50% better performance/$ vs NVDA B200/B300 in apples-to-apples FP8 AGGREGATED inference" — AND, in the same note, "GB300 NVL72 with DISAGGREGATED SERVING can outperform TPUv7 aggregated serving by ~30% perf/$ in part of the medium-latency range." The honest reading is: TPU wins ~50% aggregated-vs-aggregated, NVDA wins ~30% when allowed disaggregated serving, and SemiAnalysis expects the gap to close as Google externalises prefill/decode disaggregation, speculative decoding/MTP, KV-cache offload and agentic serving. Anyone quoting "TPU is 50% cheaper" without the serving-architecture qualifier is quoting half a sentence — recorded here so this page stops carrying the unqualified version.** 🔴 **(2) RUBIN ULTRA IS BEING RE-SPEC'D DOWN ON HBM, corroborated independently by DIGITIMES ("shifts Rubin Ultra toward 8-high HBM as memory costs rise") and by JPM's Asia team ("16-Hi HBM4E downticked to 8-Hi/12-Hi", plus SOCAMM content optimisation from 1.5TB to 768GB per CPU) — with JPM's framing that it "reflects a coping mechanism to manage insufficient supply rather than the intent to de-spec". It must not be netted against or chained into any of the other four bases.** 🔴 **BEAR MARKS LOGGED: BofA reports early Meta EMPLOYEE feedback of privacy and reliability issues and says "we are not aware of a specific product feature that will make the app go viral"; MS sales lists a "me too" pushback vs Instinct/GrokBot/GPT Work/Claude and a first-open onboarding problem; The Information expects "the only people who'll use Muse in the short term will be reporters testing it out"; and SemiAnalysis calls **Muse Spark 1.3 one of "the most clearly BENCHMAXXED models we've seen yet"** — comparable to GPT-6 and Fable 5.1 on Terminal Bench 2.1 but "markedly worse" on Terminal Bench 4.0, which is a quality challenge to the model underneath the product.** **CATALYSTS CONFIRMED: Meta Connect Sep 23-24, then Watermelon in October.** Cross-filed [[GOOG]], [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[SHOP]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).

2026-09-09⭐ KIOXIA(/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED, BUT THE PAGE GAINED THE SINGLE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL MANAGEMENT STATEMENT IN THE MEMORY COMPLEX THIS WINDOW.** 🔴 **NET-NEW: CEO Hiroo Ota, via Bloomberg, says NAND "PRICES HAVE ALREADY RISEN ENOUGH" and that another +70% q/q move is unlikely — after Kioxia's average NAND price rose **+70% q/q in the June quarter** on top of a **>100% q/q increase in the prior quarter**. This is the first time in the cycle a memory supplier has publicly named its own customer's capex budget as the reason to stop raising price, and it is the first SUPPLY-SIDE BEHAVIOURAL constraint on the bullish NAND ASP forecasts circulating in the same 48 hours (SIG blended NAND ASP +60% q/q in 3Q26 / +25% in 4Q26; UBS NAND contract +20% q/q in 3Q26 / +8% in 4Q26; JPM "elongated tightness"). It does not falsify them — holding at today's level is still a very high absolute price — but it puts a dated ceiling under the most aggressive of them, sourced to management.** **ALSO NET-NEW, HEADLINES ONLY AND NOT ADOPTED: DIGITIMES reports Kioxia "rules out deeper [[SKHYNIX]] ties"; and TrendForce/DIGITIMES report [[AAPL]] has signed a NAND long-term agreement with Kioxia, framed as "Apple yields to memory suppliers in a historic strategy shift" — which, if real, would be the first non-hyperscaler LTA of the cycle and would explain how Apple was able to price the iPhone Duo at $1,999 on 09-09 without passing the memory bill through. Apple has refused the LTA question twice on the record, so this stays unadopted. Bernstein reads it as a positive read-across to [[SNDK]].** Cross-filed [[AAPL]], [[SNDK]], [[SKHYNIX]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[MU]], [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md).
2026-09-09⭐ INTC(/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED (no house rating, PT, guide or estimate on this page changed; INTC was separately UPGRADED TO OUTPERFORM AT NORTHLAND on 09-08, logged as a third-party mark).** 🔴 **NET-NEW AND MATERIAL: (1) Intel disclosed at the SPIE Photomask + EUV conference, jointly with ASML, that HIGH-NA IS IN HIGH-VOLUME MANUFACTURING with "MORE THAN ONE MILLION WAFERS" processed across qualification, R&D and production — INCLUDING SELECT LAYERS OF PANTHER LAKE MANUFACTURED ON INTEL 18A — with registration, throughput and availability meeting expectations and the High-NA layers performing "at or better than existing 0.33-NA EUV". Intel's message is that customers need not wait for the 12'' mask format: High-NA is usable today with 6'' reticles via layout planning or stitching plus PDK support. Intel has been driving the 6x12'' large-mask standard for over three years, and ASML's CEO publicly credited its leading role since the first commercial EXE in 2024. This is the strongest technical card Intel Foundry has played in this wiki's history. (2) THE HARD COUNTER LANDED IN THE SAME WEEK, from JPM: "we do not see major front-end project wins given the technology lag (INTEL 18A COMPARABLE TO TSMC N3E)", with TSMC retaining 95%+ N2/A16 share and TSMC's N2 family reaching a $50-60bn revenue run rate by 2028 — the year Intel 14A enters mass production. 2026-09-09AMZN(/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED.** 🔴 **NET-NEW: (1) AWS named as [[QCOM]]'s third custom-silicon customer, and the STRUCTURE is what matters on this page: Amazon received **25M Qualcomm warrants at an exercise price of $161.26/share**, vesting in tranches tied to **its own product and services purchases, up to a maximum of $60bn over 10 years** — roughly 2% of Qualcomm's equity, received for becoming a customer. Barclays adds the technical fit — "AWS has used QCOM's AI100 in their EC2 instances and uses PCIe SCALE UP so this fits" — and expects the ramp to be "back end loaded like what we saw with the [[MRVL]]/[[GOOG]] deal". The pipeline-quality context matters as much: the total project pipeline **surpassed 400GW (+23GW m/m to 404GW)** but only **~35%, or 170GW, is viewed as ultimately credible** — with **hyperscaler self-builds at the HIGHEST confidence level of 79% credibility (73GW)** versus colocation at 67% (55GW), and **stranded capacity up 7GW in August to a record 12% of the pipeline**. Amazon sits in the highest-credibility bucket of a pipeline two-thirds of which is judged not credible, which is the argument for owning the self-builder over the landlord.** ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED: Bloomberg's "potentially constructing Trainium 3, shared with [[MRVL]] & Alchip" (unconfirmed, no split — it would mean three silicon partners on one programme); and EconDaily's report that AWS is increasing AI server-rack procurement, raising 3nm custom ASIC training and inference orders to [[TSM]] via Alchip with Wiwynn boosting rack/switch-tray production and Foxconn winning CPU-tray orders.** **ALSO LOGGED: [[GLW]]'s new Verizon fibre LTA explicitly cites Verizon as "the premier infrastructure partner for AWS"; AMZN Leo signed a partnership with AT&T; and Amazon launched a sterling debt offering on the day UK gilt yields hit a DMO record, read as US tech giants "increasingly diversifying into international bond markets".** Cross-filed [[QCOM]], [[MRVL]], [[ALAB]], [[GLW]], [[TSM]], [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md), [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
